0
when
\pi_{B}\rightarrow 0
for
B=1,2
, even if
F_{2D}\neq 0
. Additionally,

\mathchoice{\Gamma^{{{\pi_{3}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.3635pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.3635pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.64636pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.03311pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.62631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.62631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.04312pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.24509pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{hh}}}}{\Gamma^{{{\pi_{3}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.3635pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.3635pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.64636pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.03311pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.62631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.62631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.04312pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.24509pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{hh}}}}{\Gamma^{{{\pi_{3}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.3635pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.3635pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.64636pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.03311pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.62631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.62631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.04312pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.24509pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{hh}}}}{\Gamma^{{{\pi_{3}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.3635pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.3635pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.64636pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.03311pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.62631pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.62631pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.04312pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.24509pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{hh}}}}\rightarrow\frac{1}{2}\,\sqrt{1-\frac{\pi_{3}^{2}}{v^{2}}}\,\frac{K^{\prime}F_{2D}-2KF_{2D}^{\prime}}{K(F_{C}+F_{T})-F_{2D}^{2}}\,.

(86)

•

There are four independent sectional curvatures. At the vacuum, the curvature components reduce to

\displaystyle R_{h\pi_{1}\pi_{1}h}\,,\;

\displaystyle R_{h\pi_{2}\pi_{2}h}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle-\frac{K^{\prime}F_{C}^{\prime}}{4K}-\frac{F_{C}^{\prime 2}}{4F_{C}}+\frac{F_{C}^{\prime\prime}}{2}

(87)

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{1}{v^{2}}+\frac{K^{\prime}F_{C}^{\prime}}{4K^{2}}\right)\frac{F_{2D}^{2}}{F_{C}}+\frac{F_{C}^{\prime}\,F_{2D}^{\vphantom{{}^{\prime}}}\,F_{2D}^{\prime}}{2KF_{C}}+\ldots\,,

\displaystyle R_{h\pi_{3}\pi_{3}h}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle-\frac{K^{\prime}F_{C}^{\prime}}{4K}-\frac{F_{C}^{\prime 2}}{4F_{C}}+\frac{F_{C}^{\prime\prime}}{2}

(88)

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{K^{\prime}}{4K}+\frac{F_{C}^{\prime}}{2F_{C}}\right)F_{T}^{\prime}+\frac{F_{T}^{\prime\prime}}{2}-\frac{F_{C}^{\prime 2}\,F_{T}^{2}}{4F_{C}^{3}}+\frac{F_{C}^{\prime}\,F_{T}^{\vphantom{{}^{\prime}}}\,F_{T}^{\prime}}{2F_{C}^{2}}-\frac{F_{T}^{\prime 2}}{4F_{C}}

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{K^{\prime}}{K}+\frac{F_{C}^{\prime}}{F_{C}}\right)\frac{F_{C}^{\prime}F_{2D}^{2}}{4KF_{C}}+\frac{F_{C}^{\prime}\,F_{2D}^{\vphantom{{}^{\prime}}}\,F_{2D}^{\prime}}{2KF_{C}}+\ldots\,,

\displaystyle R_{\pi_{1}\pi_{2}\pi_{2}\pi_{1}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle-\frac{F_{C}}{v^{2}}+\frac{F_{C}^{\prime 2}}{4K}+\frac{3F_{T}}{v^{2}}+\frac{F_{C}^{\prime 2}\,F_{2D}^{2}}{4K^{2}F_{C}}+\ldots\,,

(89)

\displaystyle R_{\pi_{1}\pi_{3}\pi_{3}\pi_{1}}\,,\kern 5.0pt

\displaystyle R_{\pi_{2}\pi_{3}\pi_{3}\pi_{2}}\;

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle-\frac{F_{C}}{v^{2}}+\frac{F_{C}^{\prime 2}}{4K}-\frac{2F_{T}}{v^{2}}+\frac{F_{C}^{\prime}\,F_{T}^{\prime}}{4K}-\frac{F_{T}^{2}}{v^{2}F_{C}}+\frac{F_{C}^{\prime 2}\,F_{2D}^{2}}{4K^{2}F_{C}}+\ldots\,,

(90)

Omitted terms are at least cubic in
F_{T}
,
F_{2D}
or their derivatives. The difference between
R_{h\pi_{A}\pi_{A}h}
for
A=3
and for
A=1,2
is due to the custodial symmetry violating terms
F_{T}
and
F_{2D}
, and likewise for
R_{\pi_{A}\pi_{B}\pi_{B}\pi_{A}}
.

Consider the case
F_{2D}=0
. The line
\mathcal{H}
passing through the vacuum and parameterized by
\bm{h}
at unit speed via

\bm{h}=\int_{0}^{h}d\eta\;\sqrt{K(\eta)}\,,\quad\vec{\pi}=\vec{0}\,,

(91)

will then satisfy the geodesic equation

\frac{d^{2}\phi^{I}}{d\bm{h}^{2}}+\mathchoice{\Gamma^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.29448pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.29448pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70047pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.50032pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{JK}}}}{\Gamma^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.29448pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.29448pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70047pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.50032pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{JK}}}}{\Gamma^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.29448pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.29448pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70047pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.50032pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{JK}}}}{\Gamma^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.29448pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.29448pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.70047pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.50032pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{JK}}}}\,\frac{d\phi^{J}}{d\bm{h}}\frac{d\phi^{K}}{d\bm{h}}=0\,.

(92)

The orthonormal frame

\bigg\{\partial_{\bm{h}}\,,\,\partial_{\bm{\pi_{1}}}\,,\,\partial_{\bm{\pi_{2}}}\,,\,\partial_{\bm{\pi_{3}}}\bigg\}=\bigg\{\frac{\partial_{h}}{\sqrt{K}}\,,\,\frac{\partial_{\pi_{1}}}{\sqrt{F_{C}}}\,,\,\frac{\partial_{\pi_{2}}}{\sqrt{F_{C}}}\,,\,\frac{\partial_{\pi_{3}}}{\sqrt{F_{C}+F_{T}}}\bigg\}\,,

(93)

is covariantly constant along
\mathcal{H}
, the first of which is its tangent vector. Sending out secondary geodesics in the
\partial_{\bm{\pi_{A}}}
directions, we obtain a Fermi normal coordinate system
\phi^{\bm{I}}
adapted to the primary geodesic
\mathcal{H}
. This corresponds to a field redefinition

\displaystyle h

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{K^{1/2}}\,\bm{h}-\frac{K^{\prime}}{4K^{2}}\,\bm{h}^{2}+\frac{F_{C}^{\prime}}{4KF_{C}}\left(\bm{\pi}_{1}^{2}+\bm{\pi}_{2}^{2}\right)+\frac{F_{C}^{\prime}+F_{T}^{\prime}}{4K(F_{C}+F_{T})}\,\bm{\pi}_{3}^{2}+\ldots\,,

(94)

\displaystyle\pi_{B}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{F_{C}}^{1/2}}\,\bm{\pi_{B}}-\frac{F_{C}^{\prime}}{2K^{1/2}{F_{C}}^{3/2}}\,\bm{h}\bm{\pi_{B}}-\frac{\epsilon_{AB3}\,F_{T}}{v{F_{C}}^{3/2}(F_{C}+F_{T})^{1/2}}\,\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{\pi_{3}}+\ldots\,,

(95)

\displaystyle\pi_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(F_{C}+F_{T})^{1/2}}\,\bm{\pi_{3}}-\frac{F_{C}^{\prime}+F_{T}^{\prime}}{2K^{1/2}(F_{C}+F_{T})^{3/2}}\,\bm{h}\bm{\pi_{3}}+\ldots\,,

(96)

about the vacuum, where
B=1
or
2
,
\epsilon
is the Levi-Civita symbol and terms at first non-trivial order are shown.
17
17

17

The first deviation from Riemann normal coordinates is
\phi^{I}\sim\mathchoice{\Gamma^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[21.12976pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[21.12976pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[14.91719pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.98845pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{J}\bm{K},\,\bm{L}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[21.12976pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[21.12976pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[14.91719pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.98845pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{J}\bm{K},\,\bm{L}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[21.12976pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[21.12976pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[14.91719pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.98845pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{J}\bm{K},\,\bm{L}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[21.12976pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[21.12976pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[14.91719pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[10.98845pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\bm{J}\bm{K},\,\bm{L}}}}}\,\phi^{\bm{J}}\phi^{\bm{K}}\phi^{\bm{L}}
at the next order
Klein:2007xj
.
Each
\phi^{\bm{I}}
is a canonically normalized version of
\phi^{I}
.

The line
\mathcal{H}
is significant — it is the coordinate axis of the physical Higgs boson
\bm{h}
unconstrained by symmetry. Generally, for each value of
\bm{h}
, the geometry along the Goldstone boson directions
\bm{\pi_{A}}
must realize the
SU(2)_{L}\times U(1)_{Y}
symmetry and is hence fixed by the four values of
K
,
F_{C}
,
F_{T}
and
F_{2D}
.
18
18

18

The last is zero in the case considered here.
At the vacuum, these numbers are accessible via
e.g.
the four sectional curvatures listed above. This is in contrast with the
\bm{h}
direction, which contains not four numbers’ but four functions’ worth of information. By setting up Fermi normal coordinates aligned with
\mathcal{H}
, we are best adapted to reconstruct their functional dependence and hence any distant geometric features at finite
\bm{h}
encoded within.

In the general scenario that
F_{2D}\neq 0
, one can still probe the physical Higgs boson direction by sending out a geodesic from the vacuum with initial velocity
\partial_{\bm{h}}
. While
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
will remain at zero, mixing of
h
with
\pi_{3}
means that the latter will begin to grow as
\sim F_{2D}
. The geodesic will not deviate significantly from the
h
coordinate axis near the vacuum, in view of experimental constraints on
F_{2D}
. However, interesting geometric features typically reside at large distances
h\sim v
, and their detailed reconstruction will require knowledge of custodial symmetry violation at higher orders in the EFT expansion.
19
19

19

One can alternatively use a coordinate system derived by performing Fermi-Walker transport on an orthonormal frame along the non-geodesic
h
coordinate axis. However, like Riemann normal coordinates, it will not be locally flat along the axis.

3.2
Fermion sector

The invariant operators in the fermion sector include the Yukawa interactions

\mathcal{L}\supset-\frac{v}{\sqrt{2}}\,y(h)\left[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{L}\,U\,Q_{R}\right]-\frac{v}{2\sqrt{2}}\,y_{T}(h)\left[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{L}\,U\sigma_{3}\,Q_{R}\right]+\text{h.c.}\,,

(97)

the Hermitian kinetic terms

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2}\,k_{L}(h)\,\big[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{L}\overset{\text{\tiny$\leftrightarrow$}}{\not{\partial}}Q_{L}\big]

\displaystyle+\frac{i}{4}\,k_{LT}(h)\left[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{L}\,T\,(\not{\partial}Q_{L})-(\not{\partial}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{L})\,T\,Q_{L}\right]

(98a)

\displaystyle\quad+\,

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2}\,k_{R}(h)\,\big[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{R}\overset{\text{\tiny$\leftrightarrow$}}{\not{\partial}}Q_{R}\big]

\displaystyle+\frac{i}{4}\,k_{RT}(h)\left[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{R}\,\sigma_{3}\,(\not{\partial}Q_{R})-(\not{\partial}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{R})\,\sigma_{3}\,Q_{R}\right]\,,

(98b)

and the anti-Hermitian kinetic terms

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\frac{i}{v}\,\omega_{L}(h)\left[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{L}\,\gamma^{\mu}\,Q_{L}\right]\partial_{\mu}h

\displaystyle+\frac{i}{2v}\,\omega_{LT}(h)\left[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{L}\,\gamma^{\mu}T\,Q_{L}\right]\partial_{\mu}h

(99a)

\displaystyle\quad+\,

\displaystyle i\,\varpi_{L}(h)\left[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{L}\,\gamma^{\mu}V_{\mu}\,Q_{L}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{i}{2}\,\varpi_{LT1}(h)\left[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{L}\,(\not{\partial}T)\,Q_{L}\right]

(99b)

\displaystyle\quad+

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2}\,\varpi_{LT2}(h)\left[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{L}\,\gamma^{\mu}\,Q_{L}\right]\Tr\big[V_{\mu}T\big]

\displaystyle+\frac{i}{2}\,\varpi_{LT3}(h)\left[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{L}\,\gamma^{\mu}T\,Q_{L}\right]\Tr\big[V_{\mu}T\big]

\displaystyle\quad+

\displaystyle\frac{i}{v}\,\omega_{R}(h)\left[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{R}\,\gamma^{\mu}\,Q_{R}\right]\partial_{\mu}h

\displaystyle+\frac{i}{2v}\,\omega_{RT}(h)\left[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{R}\,\gamma^{\mu}\sigma_{3}\,Q_{R}\right]\partial_{\mu}h

(99c)

\displaystyle\quad+

\displaystyle i\,\varpi_{R}(h)\left[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{R}\,\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muU\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\gamma^{\mu}V_{\mu}U\,Q_{R}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{i}{2}\,\varpi_{RT1}(h)\left[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{R}\,\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muU\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(\not{\partial}T)U\,Q_{R}\right]

(99d)

\displaystyle\quad+

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2}\,\varpi_{RT2}(h)\left[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{R}\,\gamma^{\mu}\,Q_{R}\right]\Tr\big[V_{\mu}T\big]

\displaystyle+\frac{i}{2}\,\varpi_{RT3}(h)\left[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{R}\,\gamma^{\mu}\sigma_{3}\,Q_{R}\right]\Tr\big[V_{\mu}T\big]\,.

Note that the kinetic terms involve only fermions in left-handed or right-handed doublets, while the Yukawa terms necessarily span fermions of both chiralities. Like in the scalar sector, we expect the
y
and
k
operators labeled with
T
to be small compared to their custodially symmetric counterparts near the vacuum. While not our focus, four-fermion operators and their enumeration pose no difficulties.

Computing geometric quantities on the vector bundle
\mathcal{E}
, we obtain the fermion mass matrix

\displaystyle M_{\bar{t}_{L}t_{R}}

\displaystyle=M_{\bar{t}_{R}t_{L}}^{*}

\displaystyle=\,\frac{\sqrt{v^{2}-\vec{\pi}^{2}}+i\pi_{3}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(y+\frac{1}{2}\,y_{T}\right)\,,

(100)

\displaystyle M_{\bar{t}_{L}b_{R}}

\displaystyle=M_{\bar{b}_{R}t_{L}}^{*}

\displaystyle=\,\frac{i\pi_{1}+\pi_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(y-\frac{1}{2}\,y_{T}\right)\,,

(101)

\displaystyle M_{\bar{b}_{L}t_{R}}

\displaystyle=M_{\bar{t}_{R}b_{L}}^{*}

\displaystyle=\,\frac{i\pi_{1}-\pi_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(y+\frac{1}{2}\,y_{T}\right)\,,

(102)

\displaystyle M_{\bar{b}_{L}b_{R}}

\displaystyle=M_{\bar{b}_{R}b_{L}}^{*}

\displaystyle=\,\frac{\sqrt{v^{2}-\vec{\pi}^{2}}-i\pi_{3}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(y-\frac{1}{2}\,y_{T}\right)\,.

(103)

The Hermitian metric reads

\displaystyle k_{\bar{t}_{L}t_{L}}

\displaystyle=\,k_{L}+\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\pi_{1}^{2}+\pi_{2}^{2}}{v^{2}}\right)k_{LT}\,,

(104)

\displaystyle k_{\bar{t}_{L}b_{L}}=k_{\bar{b}_{L}t_{L}}^{*}

\displaystyle=\,-\left[i\left(\pi_{1}+\frac{\pi_{2}\pi_{3}}{\sqrt{v^{2}-\vec{\pi}^{2}}}\right)+\left(\pi_{2}-\frac{\pi_{1}\pi_{3}}{\sqrt{v^{2}-\vec{\pi}^{2}}}\right)\right]\sqrt{1-\frac{\vec{\pi}^{2}}{v^{2}}}\,\frac{k_{LT}}{v}\,,

(105)

\displaystyle k_{\bar{b}_{L}b_{L}}

\displaystyle=\,k_{L}-\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\pi_{1}^{2}+\pi_{2}^{2}}{v^{2}}\right)k_{LT}\,,

(106)

\displaystyle k_{\bar{t}_{R}t_{R}}

\displaystyle=\,k_{R}+\frac{1}{2}\,k_{RT}\,,

(107)

\displaystyle k_{\bar{b}_{R}b_{R}}

\displaystyle=\,k_{R}-\frac{1}{2}\,k_{RT}\,.

(108)

We again defer the detailed expressions of the connection and curvature to
appendix

B
, but will mention some notable features of the latter:

•

If
(\bar{p},r)
are both top or bottom components of doublets of the same chirality, then

R_{\bar{p}rh\pi_{3}}\big|\neq 0\quad\text{and}\quad R_{\bar{p}r\pi_{1}\pi_{2}}\big|\neq 0\,.

(109)

If one of
(\bar{p},r)
is a top component and the other is a bottom component, then

\displaystyle R_{\bar{p}rh\pi_{1}}\big|

\displaystyle=iR_{\bar{p}rh\pi_{2}}\big|

\displaystyle=-\,R_{\bar{r}ph\pi_{1}}^{*}\big|

\displaystyle=-\,iR_{\bar{r}ph\pi_{2}}^{*}\big|

\displaystyle\neq 0\,,

(110)

\displaystyle R_{\bar{p}r\pi_{1}\pi_{3}}\big|

\displaystyle=iR_{\bar{p}r\pi_{2}\pi_{3}}\big|

\displaystyle=-\,R_{\bar{r}p\pi_{1}\pi_{3}}^{*}\big|

\displaystyle=-\,iR_{\bar{p}r\pi_{2}\pi_{3}}^{*}\big|

\displaystyle\neq 0\,.

(111)

Otherwise, the curvature vanishes at the vacuum.

•

Custodial symmetry forbids the
\omega
’s,
i.e.
the couplings to the physical Higgs boson, from entering the curvature. More generally, the first set
eq.

109
is indeed solely determined by the
\varpi
’s,
i.e.
the couplings to the Goldstone bosons, at the vacuum. However, the
\omega
’s do arise at the vacuum in the second set,
eqs.

110
to
111
, and also beyond the vacuum in the first set for
\omega_{L}
and
\omega_{LT}
.

•

Custodial symmetry entails that
R_{\bar{t}_{L}t_{L}IJ}=-\,R_{\bar{b}_{L}b_{L}IJ}
within the first set of curvatures. This relation is violated by non-vanishing
k_{LT}
or
\varpi_{LT2}
. For example,

R_{\bar{t}_{L}t_{L}h\pi_{3}}\big|\supset\frac{{k_{L}^{+}}^{\prime}}{k_{L}^{+}}\,\frac{\varpi_{L}}{v}-\frac{\varpi_{LT2}^{\prime}}{v}\kern 5.0pt\text{is not the negative of}\kern 5.0ptR_{\bar{b}_{L}b_{L}h\pi_{3}}\big|\supset-\frac{{k_{L}^{-}}^{\prime}}{k_{L}^{-}}\,\frac{\varpi_{L}}{v}-\frac{\varpi_{LT2}^{\prime}}{v}\,,

(112)

where
k_{L}^{\pm}\coloneqq k_{L}^{\vphantom{\pm}}\pm k_{LT}^{\vphantom{\pm}}/2
. The same applies to the other doublet
L\rightarrow R
.

Writing
\omega_{L}^{\pm}=\omega_{L}^{\vphantom{\pm}}\pm\omega_{LT}^{\vphantom{\pm}}/2
and likewise for
L\rightarrow R
,
20
20

20

These combinations for custodial symmetry violation should not be confused with the
\omega^{\pm}
in
Derda:2024jvo
that defines the vector bundle connection
eq.

4
.
the fermionic Fermi normal coordinates are given by the field redefinition

\displaystyle t_{R}

\displaystyle=\left[1-\frac{\omega_{R}^{+}}{k_{R}^{+}}\,\frac{h}{v}+\frac{\varpi_{R}+\varpi_{RT2}+\varpi_{RT3}}{k_{R}^{+}}\,\frac{\pi_{3}}{v}\right]\frac{\bm{t_{R}}}{(k_{R}^{+})^{1/2}}

(113)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{i\varpi_{R}-\varpi_{RT1}}{(k_{R}^{+}\,k_{R}^{-})^{1/2}}\,\frac{i\pi_{1}+\pi_{2}}{v}\,\frac{\bm{b_{R}}}{(k_{R}^{+})^{1/2}}+\ldots\,,

\displaystyle t_{L}

\displaystyle=(R\rightarrow L)+\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{k_{LT}}{k_{L}^{+}}\,\frac{i\pi_{1}+\pi_{2}}{v}\,\frac{\bm{b_{L}}}{(k_{L}^{-})^{1/2}}+\ldots\,,

(114)

\displaystyle b_{R}

\displaystyle=\left[1-\frac{\omega_{R}^{-}}{k_{R}^{-}}\,\frac{h}{v}-\frac{\varpi_{R}-\varpi_{RT2}+\varpi_{RT3}}{k_{R}^{-}}\,\frac{\pi_{3}}{v}\right]\frac{\bm{b_{R}}}{(k_{R}^{-})^{1/2}}

(115)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{i\varpi_{R}+\varpi_{RT1}}{(k_{R}^{+}\,k_{R}^{-})^{1/2}}\,\frac{i\pi_{1}-\pi_{2}}{v}\,\frac{\bm{t_{R}}}{(k_{R}^{-})^{1/2}}+\ldots\,,

\displaystyle b_{L}

\displaystyle=(R\rightarrow L)-\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{k_{LT}}{k_{L}^{-}}\,\frac{i\pi_{1}-\pi_{2}}{v}\,\frac{\bm{t_{L}}}{(k_{L}^{+})^{1/2}}+\ldots\,,

(116)

at first non-trivial order in scalar coordinates about the vacuum. To be clear, the vector bundle connection coefficients do not affect the geodesic
\mathcal{H}
on
\mathcal{M}
used for Fermi normal coordinates.
21
21

21

If viewed from the perspective of the total space of the vector bundle,
\mathcal{H}
lies on the null section
\chi^{p}=0
and the geodesic equation is trivially satisfied in the fermionic directions.

3.3
Imprints of new physics on field space

The geometric reformulation of EFT operators in the SM Higgs sector provides a meaningful interpretation of searches for new physics, without ambiguity from field redefinitions — constraints on Wilson coefficients in the functions
K,F_{C},\ldots
are truly bounds on field space tensors like
R
. Aside from the quantitative values of these tensors, there are more general qualitative features with deeper physical implications. We now consider how such geometric information can be recovered from scattering experiments, starting with those readily accessible at the vacuum and then venturing beyond.

To identify physics beyond the SM, one must establish the geometry of the SM itself. As has been well understood, the field space of the SM is flat
Alonso:2015fsp
. This is evident once we substitute the only non-vanishing kinetic terms

\text{SM:}\quad K=1\,,\quad F_{C}=\frac{r^{2}}{v^{2}}\,,\quad k_{L}=k_{R}=1\,,

(117)

with
r=v+h
into the curvature expressions derived above. Hence, any new physics at the one- or two-derivative level is signaled by non-zero curvature.
22
22

22

Derivative field redefinitions may complicate the precise physical content encapsulated in the curvature.
At the zero-derivative level, the SM itself is already non-trivial. The scalar potential assumes a specific form

\text{SM:}\quad V=-\frac{\mu^{2}}{2}\,r^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{4}\,r^{4}\,,

(118)

and the fermion mass matrix is not invariant under
t\leftrightarrow b
— the
y
and
y_{T}
that enter
M
are non-vanishing constants with
0<|\,y_{T}/y\,|\ll 1
, representing small but non-zero custodial symmetry violation.

Any first signs of new physics will reside in four-point scattering amplitudes, which grant immediate access to the geometry at the vacuum. The relevant scalar amplitudes read

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\,\Big(\bm{\pi_{A}}\,\bm{\pi_{A}}\rightarrow\bm{h}\,\bm{h}\Big)\supset

\displaystyle-R_{\bm{h}\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{h}}\big|\left(E^{2}-2\,m_{\bm{h}}^{2}\right)-V_{;\,\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{h}\bm{h}}\big|\,,

(119)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\,\Big(\bm{\pi_{A}}\,\bm{\pi_{A}}\rightarrow\bm{\pi_{B}}\,\bm{\pi_{B}}\Big)\supset

\displaystyle-R_{\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{\pi_{B}}\bm{\pi_{B}}\bm{\pi_{A}}}\big|\left(E^{2}-\frac{2}{3}\,m_{\bm{\pi_{A}}}^{2}-\frac{2}{3}\,m_{\bm{\pi_{B}}}^{2}\right)-V_{;\,(\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{\pi_{B}}\bm{\pi_{B}})}\big|\,.

(120)

Focusing on the large momentum limit, we display terms that do not diminish with energy, and consider the Goldstone bosons
\bm{\pi_{1/2}}
and
\bm{\pi_{3}}
equivalent to the longitudinal modes of the
W^{\pm}
and
Z^{0}
bosons. Converting between Fermi and CCWZ curvature components at the vacuum amounts to appending appropriate wavefunction renormalization factors, due to the tensorial transformation laws

R_{\bm{h}\bm{\pi_{3}}\bm{\pi_{3}}\bm{h}}\big|=\left(\frac{\partial\phi^{I}}{\partial\bm{h}}\,\frac{\partial\phi^{J}}{\partial\bm{\pi_{3}}}\,\frac{\partial\phi^{K}}{\partial\bm{\pi_{3}}}\,\frac{\partial\phi^{L}}{\partial\bm{h}}\,R_{IJKL}\right)\bigg|=\frac{R_{h\pi_{3}\pi_{3}h}}{K(F_{C}+F_{T})}\bigg|\,,

(121)

and so on. Evidently, any growth of the scalar amplitudes with energy implies that the scalar field space
\mathcal{M}
is curved at the vacuum and deviates from the SM. Furthermore, if there is a difference in the rate of growth for amplitudes involving
\bm{\pi_{1/2}}
and
\bm{\pi_{3}}
, there must then be a new source of custodial symmetry violation beyond the SM, rendering
\mathcal{M}
anisotropic
Alonso:2016oah
. According to
eqs.

87
to
90
, this difference is first- and second-order in
F_{T}
and
F_{2D}
respectively, and hence a leading indicator of a CP-even origin.

Writing
P_{L}=(1-\gamma_{5})/2
for the left-handed projection operator, the relevant fermion amplitudes include

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\,\Big(\bm{\bar{t}_{L}}\,\bm{t_{L}}\rightarrow\bm{\pi_{3}}\,\bm{h}\Big)\supset

\displaystyle-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muv\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(q_{1})\left[R_{\bm{\bar{t}_{L}}\bm{t_{L}}\bm{h}\bm{\pi_{3}}}\big|\,\not{q}_{4}+M_{\bm{\bar{t}_{L}}\bm{t_{L}};\,\bm{\pi_{3}}\bm{h}}\big|\right]P_{L}\,u(q_{2})\,,

(122)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\,\Big(\bm{\bar{t}_{L}}\,\bm{t_{L}}\rightarrow\bm{\pi_{1}}\,\bm{\pi_{2}}\Big)\supset

\displaystyle-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muv\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(q_{1})\left[\frac{1}{2}\,R_{\bm{\bar{t}_{L}}\bm{t_{L}}\bm{\pi_{1}}\bm{\pi_{2}}}\big|\left(\not{q}_{3}-\not{q}_{4}\right)+M_{\bm{\bar{t}_{L}}\bm{t_{L}};\,(\bm{\pi_{1}}\bm{\pi_{2}})}\big|\right]P_{L}\,u(q_{2})\,,

(123)

and
t_{L}\rightarrow b_{L}
, which probe the first set of curvatures on the vector bundle
\mathcal{E}
. Like before, any quadratic growth of the amplitudes with energy is an indicator of non-zero curvature and hence new physics. Moreover, a difference in the cross sections under
t_{L}\leftrightarrow b_{L}
may signal a new source of custodial symmetry violation, rendering
\mathcal{E}
asymmetric in the top and bottom directions. In the high energy limit, the violation can be unequivocally attributed to non-zero
k_{LT}
or
\varpi_{LT2}
, while violation at lower energies must be in excess of the SM Yukawa couplings. Between the two choices of final states, scattering into
\bm{\pi_{1}}\bm{\pi_{2}}
also gives access to the value of
\varpi_{LT1}
at the vacuum. There are additionally the amplitudes

\mathcal{A}\,\Big(\bm{\bar{t}_{L}}\,\bm{b_{L}}\rightarrow\bm{\pi_{B}}\,\bm{h}\Big)\supset-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muv\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(q_{1})\left[R_{\bm{\bar{t}_{L}}\bm{b_{L}}\bm{h}\bm{\pi_{B}}}\big|\,\not{q}_{4}+M_{\bm{\bar{t}_{L}}\bm{b_{L}};\,\bm{\pi_{B}}\bm{h}}\big|\right]P_{L}\,u(q_{2})\,,

(124)

for
B=1,2
and
t_{L}\leftrightarrow b_{L}
, which at high energies assess the second set of curvatures and the derivative Higgs coupling
\omega_{LT}
that appear within. The same analysis holds for
L\rightarrow R
.

While amplitudes at low points provide an immediate assessment of the geometry at the vacuum and diagnose any anisotropy or asymmetry due to custodial symmetry violation, we expect that amplitudes at higher points begin to reveal any curvature singularity beyond the vacuum left behind by heavy states. This has been discussed in detail in the previous section for one of the vector bundle curvatures that enter the fermion amplitude
eq.

60
as the sole leading contribution. The high-energy behavior of the amplitude is then in direct correspondence with the Taylor coefficients of the curvature at the vacuum in Fermi normal coordinates, allowing us to estimate its behavior a finite distance away. Generally speaking, it is more difficult to extract the other field space curvatures from their corresponding amplitudes because of competing contributions from other contact and factorizable terms. For the Higgs sector specifically, it turns out that
F_{2D}=0
entails

R_{\bm{h}\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{\pi_{B}}\bm{h}}\big|_{\mathcal{H}}\propto\delta_{\bm{A}\bm{B}}\,,\kern 5.0ptR_{\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{\pi_{B}}\bm{\pi_{C}}\bm{h}}\big|_{\mathcal{H}}\propto(3\delta_{\bm{C}\bm{3}}-1)\,\epsilon_{\bm{A}\bm{B}\bm{C}}\kern 5.0pt\text{and}\kern 5.0ptR_{\bm{h}(\bm{\pi_{1}}\bm{\pi_{2}}|\bm{h};\,|\bm{\pi_{3}})}\big|_{\mathcal{H}}=0\,,

(125)

on the Higgs coordinate axis
\mathcal{H}
which is a geodesic. These identities can help eliminate some competing terms for certain theories. Consider the three-Goldstone amplitude

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\,\Big(\bm{\pi_{A}}\,\bm{\pi_{B}}\rightarrow\bm{\pi_{C}}\,\bm{h}^{N+1}\Big)\supset-\,\mathchoice{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[67.94197pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[67.94197pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[44.7126pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[37.25908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\bm{h}(\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{\pi_{B}}|\bm{h};\,|\bm{\pi_{C}})\bm{h}^{N-1}}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[67.94197pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[67.94197pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[44.7126pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[37.25908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\bm{h}(\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{\pi_{B}}|\bm{h};\,|\bm{\pi_{C}})\bm{h}^{N-1}}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[67.94197pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[67.94197pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[44.7126pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[37.25908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\bm{h}(\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{\pi_{B}}|\bm{h};\,|\bm{\pi_{C}})\bm{h}^{N-1}}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[67.94197pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[67.94197pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[44.7126pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[37.25908pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\bm{h}(\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{\pi_{B}}|\bm{h};\,|\bm{\pi_{C}})\bm{h}^{N-1}}}}}\big|\,s_{123}

(126)

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt-\frac{2}{3}\,\mathchoice{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[52.61453pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[52.61453pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[35.80254pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[30.06143pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\bm{\pi_{C}}(\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{\pi_{B}})\bm{h};\,\bm{h}^{N}}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[52.61453pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[52.61453pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[35.80254pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[30.06143pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\bm{\pi_{C}}(\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{\pi_{B}})\bm{h};\,\bm{h}^{N}}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[52.61453pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[52.61453pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[35.80254pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[30.06143pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\bm{\pi_{C}}(\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{\pi_{B}})\bm{h};\,\bm{h}^{N}}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[52.61453pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[52.61453pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[35.80254pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[30.06143pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\bm{\pi_{C}}(\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{\pi_{B}})\bm{h};\,\bm{h}^{N}}}}}\big|\,s_{12}-\frac{2}{3}\,\mathchoice{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[52.61453pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[52.61453pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[35.80254pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[30.06143pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\bm{\pi_{B}}(\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{\pi_{C}})\bm{h};\,\bm{h}^{N}}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[52.61453pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[52.61453pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[35.80254pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[30.06143pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\bm{\pi_{B}}(\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{\pi_{C}})\bm{h};\,\bm{h}^{N}}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[52.61453pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[52.61453pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[35.80254pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[30.06143pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\bm{\pi_{B}}(\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{\pi_{C}})\bm{h};\,\bm{h}^{N}}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[52.61453pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[52.61453pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[35.80254pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[30.06143pt][c]{ \displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt-\left(\mathchoice{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[47.01727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[47.01727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[32.38033pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[28.11699pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{\pi_{B}}\bm{\pi_{C}}\bm{h}\vphantom{\bm{h}^{N-2}}}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[47.01727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[47.01727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[32.38033pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[28.11699pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{\pi_{B}}\bm{\pi_{C}}\bm{h}\vphantom{\bm{h}^{N-2}}}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[47.01727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[47.01727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[32.38033pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[28.11699pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{\pi_{B}}\bm{\pi_{C}}\bm{h}\vphantom{\bm{h}^{N-2}}}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[47.01727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[47.01727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[32.38033pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[28.11699pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{\pi_{B}}\bm{\pi_{C}}\bm{h}\vphantom{\bm{h}^{N-2}}}}}}\big|\,s_{14}+\mathchoice{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[47.01727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[47.01727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[32.38033pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[28.11699pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\bm{\pi_{C}}\bm{\pi_{B}}\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{h}\vphantom{\bm{h}^{N-2}}}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[47.01727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[47.01727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[32.38033pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[28.11699pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\bm{\pi_{C}}\bm{\pi_{B}}\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{h}\vphantom{\bm{h}^{N-2}}}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[47.01727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[47.01727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[32.38033pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[28.11699pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\bm{\pi_{C}}\bm{\pi_{B}}\bm{\pi_{A}}\bm{h}\vphantom{\bm{h}^{N-2}}}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[47.01727pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[47.01727pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[32.38033pt][c]{$\scr written in Fermi normal coordinates along
\mathcal{H}
. Competing propagator terms on the third line all depend on
R_{\bm{h}\bm{\pi_{D}}\bm{\pi_{D}}\bm{h}}
on
\mathcal{H}
; for example, the diagram explicitly shown has a propagator of the same flavor as the lone Goldstone external leg
\bm{\pi_{B}}
at one of its two ends. Should
R_{\bm{h}\bm{\pi_{D}}\bm{\pi_{D}}\bm{h}}
be small relative to
R_{\bm{\pi_{D}}\bm{\pi_{E}}\bm{\pi_{E}}\bm{\pi_{D}}}
, the simplified amplitude

\mathcal{A}\,\Big(\bm{\pi_{1}}\,\bm{\pi_{2}}\rightarrow\bm{\pi_{3}}\,\bm{h}^{N+1}\Big)\approx\frac{1}{2}\,\mathchoice{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[41.72942pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[41.72942pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[28.68068pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[24.02844pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\bm{\pi_{1}}\bm{\pi_{2}}\bm{\pi_{3}}\bm{h};\,\bm{h}^{N}}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[41.72942pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[41.72942pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[28.68068pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[24.02844pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\bm{\pi_{1}}\bm{\pi_{2}}\bm{\pi_{3}}\bm{h};\,\bm{h}^{N}}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[41.72942pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[41.72942pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[28.68068pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[24.02844pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\bm{\pi_{1}}\bm{\pi_{2}}\bm{\pi_{3}}\bm{h};\,\bm{h}^{N}}}}}{R^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[41.72942pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[41.72942pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[28.68068pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[24.02844pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\bm{\pi_{1}}\bm{\pi_{2}}\bm{\pi_{3}}\bm{h};\,\bm{h}^{N}}}}}\big|\left(s_{13}-s_{23}\right)\,,

(127)

becomes a direct probe of its corresponding curvature component at high energies.
23
23

23

These curvature components are not all independent, and can be converted into one another via a polarization identity. It is not surprising that neglecting some curvature components makes the amplitude depend on fewer curvature components. The simplicity of the dependence in
eq.

127
is less trivial.
We can then also expect a simple relationship
eq.

70
between the scale of unitarity violation at moderate multiplicity
N
and any singularity of
R_{\bm{\pi_{1}}\bm{\pi_{2}}\bm{\pi_{3}}\bm{h}}
along
\mathcal{H}
. Roughly speaking, such theories must be more strongly curved in the
S^{3}
along the Goldstone directions than in the
\mathbb{R}
along the Higgs direction. Outside of such situations, numerical techniques may be required to precisely separate the competing contributions
Cohen:2021ucp
. Nevertheless, by rendering
\mathcal{H}
a geodesic,
F_{2D}=0
remains a useful condition that aligns scattering processes with an increasing number of Higgs states directly with the geometry along the Higgs axis. Otherwise, when measuring any proper distance with respect to the vacuum, one must account for deviations that are hard to nail down with perturbative amplitudes.

For concreteness, consider extending the SM with a real
SU(2)_{L}
triplet scalar
\Phi_{A}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{UV}}

\displaystyle\supset-\,V_{\text{UV}}+|\partial H|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\,(\partial\Phi)^{2}\,,

(128)

\displaystyle V_{\text{UV}}

\displaystyle=-\,\mu^{2}\,|H|^{2}+\lambda\,|H|^{4}-\frac{\mu_{\Phi}^{2}}{2}\,\Phi^{2}+\frac{\lambda_{\Phi}}{4}\,\Phi^{4}-\kappa\,|H|^{2}\,\Phi^{2}-\eta\,\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muH\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\sigma_{A}H\,\Phi_{A}\,,

(129)

where we parameterize
\Phi_{A}
in the unitary basis as
Cohen:2020xca

\vec{\,\Phi}=\frac{2f}{r^{2}}\,\exp\begin{pmatrix}0&0&\beta_{2}\\
0&0&-\beta_{1}\\
-\beta_{2}&\beta_{1}&0\end{pmatrix}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muH\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\vec{\sigma}H\,.

(130)

This field leaves the fermion sector untouched, but introduces new custodial symmetry violation in the scalar sector due to the coupling
\eta
. We take the constants to satisfy
\lambda,\lambda_{\Phi},\eta>0
and
|\kappa|<\sqrt{\lambda\lambda_{\Phi}}
, ensuring in particular that
V_{\text{UV}}
is bounded below and that the cubic polynomial

-\frac{\eta}{2}\,r^{2}-(\kappa r^{2}+\mu_{\Phi}^{2})\,f+\lambda_{\Phi}\,f^{3}=0\,,

(131)

has a positive solution
f(r)>0
. Following
Cohen:2020xca
, we integrate out
\Phi_{A}
at tree level to get the EFT
24
24

24

Compared to Eq. (7.47) in
Cohen:2020xca
, we find the opposite sign for the coefficient of the
(\partial|H|^{2})^{2}
term; as a result
K
depends only on
f^{\prime}
and not
f
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\supset

\displaystyle-V+\frac{1}{2}\,(1+f^{\prime 2})\,\partial_{\mu}h\,\partial^{\mu}h+\frac{1}{4}\,(r^{2}+4f^{2})\Tr\left[V_{\mu}V^{\mu}\right]-\frac{1}{2}\,f^{2}\Tr\left[V_{\mu}T\right]\Tr\left[V^{\mu}T\right]\,,

(132)

\displaystyle V=

\displaystyle-\frac{\mu^{2}}{2}\,r^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{4}\,r^{4}-\frac{\mu_{\Phi}^{2}}{2}\,f^{2}+\frac{\lambda_{\Phi}}{4}\,f^{4}-\frac{\kappa}{2}\,r^{2}f^{2}-\frac{\eta}{2}\,r^{2}f\,.

(133)

Its kinetic terms read

K=1+f^{\prime 2}\,,\quad F_{C}=\frac{r^{2}+4f^{2}}{v^{2}}\,,\quad F_{T}=-\frac{4f^{2}}{v^{2}}\,,\quad F_{2D}=0\,,\quad k_{L}=k_{R}=1\,,

(134)

and determine the field space curvatures to be

\displaystyle R_{\bm{h}\bm{\pi_{1}}\bm{\pi_{1}}\bm{h}}\big|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle R_{\bm{h}\bm{\pi_{2}}\bm{\pi_{2}}\bm{h}}\big|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1+f^{\prime 2})\,(r^{2}+4f^{2})}\left[\frac{(4f-rf^{\prime})\,f^{\prime\prime}}{1+f^{\prime 2}}+\frac{4(f-rf^{\prime})^{2}}{r^{2}+4f^{2}}\right]\,,

(135)

\displaystyle R_{\bm{h}\bm{\pi_{3}}\bm{\pi_{3}}\bm{h}}\big|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=-\,\frac{f^{\prime}f^{\prime\prime}}{r\,(1+f^{\prime 2})^{2}}\,,

(136)

\displaystyle R_{\bm{\pi_{1}}\bm{\pi_{2}}\bm{\pi_{2}}\bm{\pi_{1}}}\big|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=-\,\frac{(4f-rf^{\prime})^{2}}{(1+f^{\prime 2})\,(r^{2}+4f^{2})^{2}}\,,

(137)

\displaystyle R_{\bm{\pi_{1}}\bm{\pi_{3}}\bm{\pi_{3}}\bm{\pi_{1}}}\big|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\;=\;

\displaystyle R_{\bm{\pi_{2}}\bm{\pi_{3}}\bm{\pi_{3}}\bm{\pi_{2}}}\big|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r^{2}+4f^{2}}\left[\frac{r+4ff^{\prime}}{r\,(1+f^{\prime 2})}-\frac{r^{2}}{r^{2}+4f^{2}}\right]\,,

(138)

\displaystyle R_{\bm{\bar{p}}\bm{r}\bm{I}\bm{J}}\big|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=\,0\,,

(139)

where we display their expressions restricted to
\mathcal{H}
for brevity. In particular, at the vacuum
\bm{h}=0
on
\mathcal{H}
, we see that the four-point scalar amplitudes will identify a non-zero
F_{T}
from the differences between
\bm{\pi_{1/2}}
and
\bm{\pi_{3}}
, while the fermionic ones will remain unchanged from the SM. This corroborates with the source of new physics, a CP-even heavy field that couples not to the SM fermions, but to the SM scalars and with additional custodial symmetry violation. Away from
\bm{h}=0
, the triplet generates singularities on
\mathcal{M}
of the form
eq.

65
that arise when
K
,
F_{C}
,
F_{C}+F_{T}
or the discriminant of the cubic polynomial vanishes. We can hence expect scalar amplitudes with a larger but finite number of Higgs states to be sensitive to these singularities as measured along the geodesic
\mathcal{H}
. This is subject to the various complications already discussed, with some possible simplifications when
\eta
and
\mu_{\Phi}
are small and
f
becomes mostly linear in
r
, so that the four-Goldstone curvatures dominate the two-Goldstone ones. Meanwhile, higher-point fermionic amplitudes will not grow with energy just like in the SM, respecting perturbative unitarity and reflecting the absence of singularities in the curvatures on
\mathcal{E}
.

4
Conclusion and outlook

Field space geometry is an important theoretical framework for parameterizing the space of effective field theories, and a useful practical tool for extracting physical meaning from experimental measurements. This work refines the general geometric construction for scalar and fermionic degrees of freedom and develops further machinery that clarifies the geometric implications of physical observables. Concretely, we supply a fully covariant formulation of fermionic field space as a vector bundle, and establish Fermi normal coordinates as a direct link between scattering amplitudes and geometric features along a geodesic. We then focus on the Higgs sector and impose the necessary symmetries on the geometry. Attention is given to custodial symmetry violation, the geometric deformations it introduces in the Goldstone boson and fermion directions, and the complications it may bring to geometric reconstruction in the Higgs boson direction. Overall, this work serves to bridge existing gaps in geometric formalism and align it closer with phenomenological applications.

An avenue for future work is the incorporation of more particles. Leptons or multiple fermion generations can be readily accommodated in the present framework, and any interesting flavor structure will arise as non-diagonality in the fermionic directions. Meanwhile, the general treatment of gauge bosons requires extensions of field space with additional degrees of freedom. Other extensions in formalism include the geometric treatment of more general field redefinitions
Cohen:2022uuw
;
Cheung:2022vnd
;
Craig:2023wni
;
Craig:2023hhp
;
Alminawi:2023qtf
;
Cohen:2023ekv
;
Cohen:2024bml
;
Lee:2024xqa
;
Cohen:2024fak
, loop effects
Alonso:2022ffe
;
Helset:2022pde
;
Assi:2023zid
;
Jenkins:2023rtg
;
Jenkins:2023bls
;
Gattus:2024ird
;
Li:2024ciy
;
Aigner:2025xyt
;
Cohen:2025dex
;
Assi:2025fsm
or other EFT operators
Assi:2023zid
;
Derda:2024jvo
;
Lee:2024xqa
;
Assi:2025fsm
, which have been explored in the literature to various extents. Access to geometry is no less important than the geometry itself, and experimentally viable probes of field space also deserve further study, such as scattering amplitudes and their intertwining of field space with kinematics
Cheung:2021yog
;
Derda:2024jvo
;
Cohen:2025dex
. The possibility of more exotic geometric features away from the perturbative vacuum and their imprints on physical observables
Alonso:2023jsi
;
Cheung:2024wme
suggest alternative prospects for physics beyond the Standard Model and opportunities for discovery.

Appendix A
A formula for phase space integration

Given
N+2
distinguishable massless states, we derive the closed-form formula
eq.

67
, with
\rho_{N+2}
and
\alpha_{N+2}
set to unity, for the phase space integral of the product of two of their momenta. The form

\int d\mathrm{LIPS}\;q_{\bm{I_{J}}}^{\mu}\,q_{\bm{I_{K}}}^{\vphantom{\mu}\nu}=E^{2N}\left(a_{N+2}\,Q^{\mu}\,Q^{\nu}+b_{N+2}\,E^{2}\,\eta^{\mu\nu}\right)\,,

(140)

is fixed by Lorentz symmetry and dimensional analysis, with constants
a_{N+2}
and
b_{N+2}
to be determined. We take
\bm{I_{J}}
and
\bm{I_{K}}
to be among the first two states without loss of generality. Using the identity
Kleiss:1985gy

1=\int d^{4}L\;\delta^{(4)}\left(L^{\rho}-\sum_{\bm{J}=\bm{1}}^{\bm{N+1}}q_{\bm{I_{J}}}^{\rho}\right)\int dW^{2}\;\delta\left(L^{2}-W^{2}\right)\,,

(141)

we decompose the
(N+2)
-state integral as

E^{2N}\left(a_{N+2}\,Q^{\mu}\,Q^{\nu}+b_{N+2}\,E^{2}\,\eta^{\mu\nu}\right)\\
=\int_{0}^{E^{2}}\frac{dW^{2}}{2\pi}\int d\mathrm{LIPS}_{2}\;W^{2(N-1)}\left(a_{N+1}\,L^{\mu}\,L^{\nu}+b_{N+1}\,W^{2}\,\eta^{\mu\nu}\right)\,,

(142)

where

d\mathrm{LIPS}_{2}=\frac{d^{3}q_{\bm{I_{N+2}}}}{(2\pi)^{3}\,2E_{\bm{I_{N+2}}}}\times d^{4}L\times 2\pi\times\delta\left(L^{2}-W^{2}\right)\times\delta^{(4)}\left(Q^{\rho}-L^{\rho}-q_{\bm{I_{N+2}}}^{\rho}\right)\,.

(143)

The variables
L
and
W
represent the total momentum and energy of the first
N+1
states. We now have a two-state integral, over
L
and
q_{\bm{I_{N+2}}}
, of an
(N+1)
-state integral of the same form. Evaluating the two-state integral yields the recursion relations

\displaystyle a_{N+2}

\displaystyle=\frac{a_{N+1}}{16\pi^{2}N(N+3)}\,,

(144)

\displaystyle b_{N+2}

\displaystyle=\frac{b_{N+1}}{16\pi^{2}(N+1)(N+2)}-\frac{a_{N+1}}{32\pi^{2}N(N+1)(N+2)(N+3)}\,,

(145)

with the solutions

a_{N+2}=\frac{6\,a_{0}}{(4\pi)^{2N}N!\,(N+3)!}\,,\quad b_{N+2}=\frac{(2N+6)\,b_{0}-Na_{0}}{(4\pi)^{2N}(N+1)!\,(N+3)!}\,.

(146)

The seeds

\left(a_{0},b_{0}\right)=\begin{cases}\left(+\frac{1}{24\pi},\,-\frac{1}{96\pi}\right)&\text{if }\bm{J}=\bm{K}\\
\left(+\frac{1}{48\pi},\,+\frac{1}{96\pi}\right)&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\,,

(147)

at
N=0
then give the desired result.

Appendix B
A compilation of geometric expressions

Calculating the field space geometry of the SM Higgs sector is straightforward in principle but rather unwieldy in practice. We begin with the scalar base space
\mathcal{M}
. For our purposes, it suffices to take the Goldstone boson coordinates
\pi_{A}
and the custodial symmetry violating terms
F_{T}
and
F_{2D}
to be small — after all, scattering amplitudes grant us access primarily to the vacuum. With this simplification and the shorthands

\xi_{A},\,\zeta_{A}=\begin{cases}\phantom{-}\,\pi_{2}/v&\text{for }A=1\\
-\,\pi_{1}/v&\text{for }A=2\\
-\,1,\,0&\text{for }\xi_{3},\,\zeta_{3}\end{cases}\quad\text{and}\quad c_{AB}=\begin{cases}\phantom{-}\,2&\text{if }(A,B)=(1,3)\text{ or }(2,3)\\
-\,2&\text{if }(A,B)=(1,2)\text{ or }(2,1)\\
\phantom{-}\,0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\,,

(148)

we list the leading terms in each Levi-Civita connection coefficient:

(159)

where an entry of
\zeta_{3}
means that the leading term is quadratic in
\pi_{A}
. Working to this order yields the curvature tensor strictly at the vacuum, which has been listed in
eqs.

87
to
90
. We note that should
F_{2D}
happen to vanish, the curvature tensor along the entire coordinates axis
\mathcal{H}
of
h
has the compact expression

\displaystyle R_{h\pi_{1}\pi_{1}h}\big|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle R_{h\pi_{2}\pi_{2}h}\big|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{K^{\prime}F_{C}^{\prime}}{4K}-\frac{F_{C}^{\prime 2}}{4F_{C}}+\frac{F_{C}^{\prime\prime}}{2}\,,

(160)

\displaystyle R_{h\pi_{3}\pi_{3}h}\big|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{K^{\prime}(F_{C}^{\prime}+F_{T}^{\prime})}{4K}-\frac{(F_{C}^{\prime}+F_{T}^{\prime})^{2}}{4(F_{C}+F_{T})}+\frac{F_{C}^{\prime\prime}+F_{T}^{\prime\prime}}{2}\,,

(161)

\displaystyle R_{\pi_{1}\pi_{2}\pi_{2}\pi_{1}}\big|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{F_{C}}{v^{2}}+\frac{F_{C}^{\prime 2}}{4K}+\frac{3F_{T}}{v^{2}}\,,

(162)

\displaystyle R_{\pi_{1}\pi_{3}\pi_{3}\pi_{1}}\big|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\;=\;

\displaystyle R_{\pi_{2}\pi_{3}\pi_{3}\pi_{2}}\big|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{F_{C}}{v^{2}}+\frac{F_{C}^{\prime 2}}{4K}-\frac{2F_{T}}{v^{2}}+\frac{F_{C}^{\prime}\,F_{T}^{\prime}}{4K}-\frac{F_{T}^{2}}{v^{2}F_{C}}\,,

(163)

and a non-independent curvature component

R_{\pi_{1}\pi_{2}\pi_{3}h}\big|_{\mathcal{H}}=-\frac{F_{C}^{\prime}\,F_{T}^{\vphantom{{}^{\prime}}}}{vF_{C}}+\frac{F_{T}^{\prime}}{v}\,.

(164)

Next is the fermion vector bundle
\mathcal{E}
, which is diagonal in chirality in the sense that any mixed component of a geometric quantity between the two
Q_{L}
and
Q_{R}
doublets vanishes. We continue to treat
\pi_{A}
as small. The leading terms of the connection coefficients in
h

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\Gamma^{{{b_{L}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.48187pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.48187pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.6945pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.39091pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.35138pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.35138pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98485pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.38402pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{3}t_{L}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{b_{L}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.48187pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.48187pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.6945pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.39091pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.35138pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.35138pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98485pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.38402pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{3}t_{L}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{b_{L}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.48187pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.48187pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.6945pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.39091pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.35138pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.35138pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98485pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.38402pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{3}t_{L}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{b_{L}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.48187pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.48187pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.6945pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.39091pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.35138pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.35138pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.98485pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.38402pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{3}t_{L}}}}}

\displaystyle\supset\bigg[-\frac{k_{LT}}{vk_{L}^{+}k_{L}^{-}}\left(\varpi_{L}+\varpi_{LT2}+\varpi_{LT3}\right)

(183)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2vk_{L}^{-}}\left(2\varpi_{L}+ik_{LT}+2i\varpi_{LT1}+4\varpi_{LT3}\right)\bigg]\left(\zeta_{1}+i\zeta_{2}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\Gamma^{{{t_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.33057pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.9317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95398pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44843pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{1}b_{R}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{t_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.33057pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.9317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95398pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44843pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{1}b_{R}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{t_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.33057pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.9317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95398pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44843pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{1}b_{R}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{t_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.33057pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.9317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95398pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44843pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{1}b_{R}}}}}

\displaystyle\supset-\frac{1}{vk_{R}^{+}}\left(\varpi_{R}+i\varpi_{RT1}\right)\,,

(184)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\Gamma^{{{b_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.9971pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.69351pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.28745pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68661pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{1}t_{R}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{b_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.9971pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.69351pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.28745pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68661pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{1}t_{R}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{b_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.9971pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.69351pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.28745pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68661pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{1}t_{R}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{b_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.9971pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.69351pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.28745pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68661pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{1}t_{R}}}}}

\displaystyle\supset-\frac{1}{vk_{R}^{-}}\left(\varpi_{R}-i\varpi_{RT1}\right)\,,

(185)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\Gamma^{{{t_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.33057pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.9317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95398pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44843pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{2}b_{R}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{t_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.33057pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.9317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95398pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44843pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{2}b_{R}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{t_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.33057pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.9317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95398pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44843pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{2}b_{R}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{t_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.33057pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.9317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95398pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44843pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{2}b_{R}}}}}

\displaystyle\supset\frac{i}{vk_{R}^{+}}\left(\varpi_{R}+i\varpi_{RT1}\right)\,,

(186)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\Gamma^{{{b_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.9971pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.69351pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.28745pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68661pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{2}t_{R}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{b_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.9971pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.69351pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.28745pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68661pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{2}t_{R}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{b_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.9971pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.69351pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.28745pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68661pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{2}t_{R}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{b_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.9971pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.69351pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.28745pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68661pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{2}t_{R}}}}}

\displaystyle\supset-\frac{i}{vk_{R}^{-}}\left(\varpi_{R}-i\varpi_{RT1}\right)\,,

(187)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\Gamma^{{{t_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.33057pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.9317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95398pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44843pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{3}b_{R}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{t_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.33057pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.9317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95398pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44843pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{3}b_{R}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{t_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.33057pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.9317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95398pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44843pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{3}b_{R}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{t_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.40132pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.33057pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.9317pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.27919pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95398pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44843pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{3}b_{R}}}}}

\displaystyle\supset-\frac{1}{vk_{R}^{+}}\left(\varpi_{R}+i\varpi_{RT1}\right)\left(\zeta_{1}-i\zeta_{2}\right)\,,

(188)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\Gamma^{{{b_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.9971pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.69351pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.28745pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68661pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{3}t_{R}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{b_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.9971pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.69351pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.28745pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68661pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{3}t_{R}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{b_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.9971pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.69351pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.28745pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68661pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{3}t_{R}}}}}{\Gamma^{{{b_{R}}\mathchoice{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[14.9055pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.9971pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.69351pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.77501pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.28745pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68661pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\pi_{3}t_{R}}}}}

\displaystyle\supset-\frac{1}{vk_{R}^{-}}\left(\varpi_{R}-i\varpi_{RT1}\right)\left(\zeta_{1}+i\zeta_{2}\right)\,.

(189)

The coefficients for the conjugate connection are complex conjugates of the above. We obtain the two sets of two-fermion curvature components

\displaystyle R_{\bar{t}_{L}t_{L}h\pi_{3}}\big|

\displaystyle=\frac{{k_{L}^{+}}^{\prime}}{vk_{L}^{+}}\left(\varpi_{L}+\varpi_{LT2}+\varpi_{LT3}\right)-\frac{1}{v}\left(\varpi_{L}^{\prime}+\varpi_{LT2}^{\prime}+\varpi_{LT3}^{\prime}\right)\,,

(190)

\displaystyle R_{\bar{t}_{R}t_{R}h\pi_{3}}\big|

\displaystyle=\frac{{k_{R}^{+}}^{\prime}}{vk_{R}^{+}}\left(\varpi_{R}+\varpi_{RT2}+\varpi_{RT3}\right)-\frac{1}{v}\left(\varpi_{R}^{\prime}+\varpi_{RT2}^{\prime}+\varpi_{RT3}^{\prime}\right)\,,

(191)

\displaystyle R_{\bar{b}_{L}b_{L}h\pi_{3}}\big|

\displaystyle=-\frac{{k_{L}^{-}}^{\prime}}{vk_{L}^{-}}\left(\varpi_{L}-\varpi_{LT2}+\varpi_{LT3}\right)+\frac{1}{v}\left(\varpi_{L}^{\prime}-\varpi_{LT2}^{\prime}+\varpi_{LT3}^{\prime}\right)\,,

(192)

\displaystyle R_{\bar{b}_{R}b_{R}h\pi_{3}}\big|

\displaystyle=-\frac{{k_{R}^{-}}^{\prime}}{vk_{R}^{-}}\left(\varpi_{R}-\varpi_{RT2}+\varpi_{RT3}\right)+\frac{1}{v}\left(\varpi_{R}^{\prime}-\varpi_{RT2}^{\prime}+\varpi_{RT3}^{\prime}\right)\,,

(193)

\displaystyle R_{\bar{t}_{L}t_{L}\pi_{1}\pi_{2}}\big|

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{v^{2}}\left(\varpi_{L}-\varpi_{LT2}-\varpi_{LT3}\right)+\frac{i}{2v^{2}k_{L}^{-}}\left[\left(2\varpi_{L}-ik_{LT}\right)^{2}+4\varpi_{LT1}^{2}\right]\,,

(194)

\displaystyle R_{\bar{t}_{R}t_{R}\pi_{1}\pi_{2}}\big|

\displaystyle=-\frac{2}{v^{2}}\left(\varpi_{R}+\varpi_{RT2}+\varpi_{RT3}\right)+\frac{i}{2v^{2}k_{R}^{-}}\left[4\varpi_{R}^{2}+4\varpi_{RT1}^{2}\right]\,,

(195)

\displaystyle R_{\bar{b}_{L}b_{L}\pi_{1}\pi_{2}}\big|

\displaystyle=-\frac{2}{v^{2}}\left(\varpi_{L}+\varpi_{LT2}-\varpi_{LT3}\right)-\frac{i}{2v^{2}k_{L}^{+}}\left[\left(2\varpi_{L}+ik_{LT}\right)^{2}+4\varpi_{LT1}^{2}\right]\,,

(196)

\displaystyle R_{\bar{b}_{R}b_{R}\pi_{1}\pi_{2}}\big|

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{v^{2}}\left(\varpi_{R}-\varpi_{RT2}+\varpi_{RT3}\right)-\frac{i}{2v^{2}k_{R}^{+}}\left[4\varpi_{R}^{2}+4\varpi_{RT1}^{2}\right]\,,

(197)

and

\displaystyle R_{\bar{t}_{L}b_{L}h\pi_{1}}\big|

\displaystyle=iR_{\bar{t}_{L}b_{L}h\pi_{2}}\big|=-R_{\bar{b}_{L}t_{L}h\pi_{1}}^{*}\big|=-iR_{\bar{b}_{L}t_{L}h\pi_{2}}^{*}\big|

(198)

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{v^{2}}\,\omega_{LT}-\frac{1}{v}\left(\varpi_{L}^{\prime}+i\varpi_{LT1}^{\prime}\right)+\frac{1}{4vk_{L}^{+}}\left({k_{L}^{+}}^{\prime}-\frac{2}{v}\,\omega_{L}^{+}\right)\left(2\varpi_{L}+ik_{LT}+2i\varpi_{LT1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{4vk_{L}^{-}}\left({k_{L}^{-}}^{\prime}+\frac{2}{v}\,\omega_{L}^{-}\right)\left(2\varpi_{L}-ik_{LT}+2i\varpi_{LT1}\right)\,,

\displaystyle R_{\bar{t}_{R}b_{R}h\pi_{1}}\big|

\displaystyle=iR_{\bar{t}_{R}b_{R}h\pi_{2}}\big|=-R_{\bar{b}_{R}t_{R}h\pi_{1}}^{*}\big|=-iR_{\bar{b}_{R}t_{R}h\pi_{2}}^{*}\big|

(199)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{v}\left(\varpi_{R}^{\prime}+i\varpi_{RT1}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{4v}\left[\frac{1}{k_{R}^{+}}\left({k_{R}^{+}}^{\prime}-\frac{2}{v}\,\omega_{R}^{+}\right)+\frac{1}{k_{R}^{-}}\left({k_{R}^{-}}^{\prime}+\frac{2}{v}\,\omega_{R}^{-}\right)\right]\left(2\varpi_{R}+2i\varpi_{RT1}\right)\,,

\displaystyle R_{\bar{t}_{L}b_{L}\pi_{1}\pi_{3}}\big|

\displaystyle=iR_{\bar{t}_{L}b_{L}\pi_{2}\pi_{3}}\big|=-R_{\bar{b}_{L}t_{L}\pi_{1}\pi_{3}}^{*}\big|=-iR_{\bar{b}_{L}t_{L}\pi_{2}\pi_{3}}^{*}\big|

(200)

\displaystyle=\frac{2i}{v^{2}}\left(\varpi_{L}+\varpi_{LT3}\right)-\frac{1}{2v^{2}k_{L}^{+}}\left(\varpi_{L}+\varpi_{LT2}+\varpi_{LT3}\right)\left(2\varpi_{L}+ik_{LT}+2i\varpi_{LT1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2v^{2}k_{L}^{-}}\left(\varpi_{L}-\varpi_{LT2}+\varpi_{LT3}\right)\left(2\varpi_{L}-ik_{LT}+2i\varpi_{LT1}\right)\,,

\displaystyle R_{\bar{t}_{R}b_{R}\pi_{1}\pi_{3}}\big|

\displaystyle=iR_{\bar{t}_{R}b_{R}\pi_{2}\pi_{3}}\big|=-R_{\bar{b}_{R}t_{R}\pi_{1}\pi_{3}}^{*}\big|=-iR_{\bar{b}_{R}t_{R}\pi_{2}\pi_{3}}^{*}\big|

(201)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2i}{v^{2}}\left(\varpi_{R}+i\varpi_{RT1}\right)-\frac{1}{2v^{2}}\bigg[\frac{1}{k_{R}^{+}}\left(\varpi_{R}+\varpi_{RT2}+\varpi_{RT3}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 130.0002pt+\frac{1}{k_{R}^{-}}\left(\varpi_{R}-\varpi_{RT2}+\varpi_{RT3}\right)\bigg]\left(2\varpi_{R}+2i\varpi_{RT1}\right)\,,

that are non-vanishing at the vacuum.

We do not distinguish the
\Gamma
’s and
R
’s of the graded Riemannian metric
G
from their underlying counterparts on
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{E}
, which differ only by
\mathcal{O}(\chi^{2})
corrections.
